+## 2026-09-01 — Gradle Daemon Memory Exhaustion
+**Type:** GOTCHA **Scope:** PROJECT **Confidence:** VERIFIED **Source:** AGENTS.md **Status:** ACTIVE
+### What
+Gradle daemon crashes due to memory limits in container; always pass `--no-daemon` to gradle wrapper (`./gradlew`) to prevent daemon memory exhaustion.
+### Why
+Prevent container out-of-memory crashes during builds.
+### Evidence
+Documented in AGENTS.md Section 4 and test_memory_compiler.py.
+### Notes
+Verified in devcontainer builds.
+

+- Running Android unit tests (`testDebugUnitTest`) or Android Lint (`lintDebug`).
+- Diagnosing Android build failures, memory issues, or dependency conflicts.
+- Connecting to headless ADB instances, running logcat, or installing packages.

+    ActionType -- "Lint Audit" --> RunLint[./gradlew lintDebug --no-daemon]

+    RunBuild --> CheckResult{Build Success?}
+    RunTest --> CheckResult
+    RunLint --> CheckResult
+    
+    CheckResult -- Failure --> DiagnoseFail[Diagnose OOM / Missing Deps / API mismatch]
+    CheckResult -- Success --> VerifyArtifact[Verify Output APK / Test Reports]
+    DiagnoseFail --> Remediate[Apply Fix & Re-run]
+    VerifyArtifact --> Done([Complete Task])
+```

 # Clean build
-./gradlew clean

-# Build debug APK

+./gradlew clean --no-daemon

-## 5. Device & Emulator Debugging
-
-For connected headless devices or emulators:
+### Workflow 4: Headless ADB & Device Interaction
 ```bash
-# List devices
-adb devices
+# Enumerate connected physical or virtual devices
+adb devices -l
 
 # Install debug APK

-# Capture logcat

-## 6. Verification & Failure Recovery
-
-- **SDK Package Missing**: Use `sdkmanager "platforms;android-34" "build-tools;34.0.0"` to install missing components.
-- **Gradle Daemon Out of Memory**: Add `org.gradle.jvmargs=-Xmx2048m -XX:MaxMetaspaceSize=512m` to `gradle.properties`.
-- **KVM / Virtualization Unavailable**: Avoid launching hardware emulators; rely on unit tests, headless Roborazzi/Robolectric, or mock runner tests.
+## 6. Verification & Validation Steps
+- **APK Generation**: Ensure target APK exists and is non-empty (`app/build/outputs/apk/debug/app-debug.apk`).
+- **Test Results**: Check test execution summary for zero failures in `app/build/reports/tests/`.
+- **Lint Integrity**: Ensure no `Fatal` or `Error` severity lint issues in `app/build/reports/lint-results-debug.xml`.
+- **Package Inspection**: Inspect APK package name and version with `aapt dump badging <apk>` or `apkanalyzer`.
+
+## 7. Failure Recovery & Troubleshooting
+- **Missing SDK Platforms or Build Tools**:
+  ```bash
+  sdkmanager "platforms;android-34" "build-tools;34.0.0"
+  ```
+- **Gradle Daemon Out of Memory / Container Kill**:
+  Add explicit heap limits to `gradle.properties`:
+  ```properties
+  org.gradle.jvmargs=-Xmx2048m -XX:MaxMetaspaceSize=512m
+  ```
+- **Virtualization / KVM Unavailable in Headless Container**:
+  Avoid launching hardware-accelerated QEMU emulators; rely on Robolectric/Roborazzi JVM-based tests or mock runners.
+- **Dependency Resolution Failure**:
+  Run `./gradlew build --refresh-dependencies --no-daemon` or inspect offline cache.
+
+## 8. Security Boundaries & Operational Guardrails
+- **Keystore Isolation**: Never hardcode production release keystore passwords, private aliases, or secrets into `build.gradle`. Always use environment variables or debug keystores for local testing.
+- **Manifest Audit**: Review `AndroidManifest.xml` for dangerous exported components (`android:exported="true"`) without permission checks.
+- **Clean Execution**: Use `--no-daemon` to avoid orphaned Java processes lingering in background.

 # Android Reverse Engineering Skill
 
-This skill provides comprehensive procedures for static and dynamic analysis, decompilation, byte-code inspection, repacking, and instrumentation of Android binaries.
+## 1. Purpose & Scope
+This skill provides comprehensive instructions for static and dynamic analysis, decompilation, bytecode disassembly, manifest analysis, patching, repackaging, and instrumentation of Android application packages (`.apk`, `.aar`, `.dex`).
 
-## 1. Toolchain Inventory & Verification
+## 2. Activation Triggers
+Activate this skill when:
+- Decompiling APKs to human-readable Java source via JADX.
+- Unpacking APK resources and Dalvik bytecode into Smali via Apktool.
+- Modifying Smali instructions or `AndroidManifest.xml` and repacking.
+- Aligning and signing modified APKs with `zipalign` and `apksigner`.
+- Performing dynamic instrumentation or API tracing via Frida on Android targets.
 
-Verify reverse engineering binaries:
+## 3. Prerequisites & Toolchain Verification
+Verify that required reverse engineering binaries and Java runtimes are present:
 ```bash
+# Verify reverse engineering binaries
 apktool -version
 jadx --version
 zipalign --version || true
 apksigner --version || true
+baksmali --version || true
 frida --version || true
 ```
 
-## 2. Static Analysis & Decompilation
+## 4. Decision Tree & Workflow Routing
+```mermaid
+flowchart TD
+    Start([APK Analysis Request]) --> TargetType{Select Objective}
+    
+    TargetType -- "Source Code Audit" --> JADXDecompile[Run JADX Decompiler]
+    TargetType -- "Resource / Smali Patch" --> ApktoolUnpack[Run Apktool Unpack]
+    TargetType -- "DEX Bytecode Only" --> BaksmaliUnpack[Extract & Baksmali DEX]
+    TargetType -- "Dynamic Tracing" --> FridaHook[Run Frida Tracing Script]
+    
+    JADXDecompile --> AuditJava[Inspect Java Source & API Contracts]
+    BaksmaliUnpack --> AuditSmali[Inspect Dalvik Opcodes]
+    
+    ApktoolUnpack --> EditFiles[Patch Smali / Manifest / Resources]
+    EditFiles --> RebuildAPK[apktool b -o unsigned.apk]
+    RebuildAPK --> AlignAPK[zipalign -p -f -v 4]
+    AlignAPK --> SignAPK[apksigner sign with debug key]
+    SignAPK --> VerifySign{apksigner verify}
+    
+    VerifySign -- Fail --> FixKeystore[Fix Key / Certificate Params]
+    VerifySign -- Pass --> CompleteOutput[Output Signed Repackaged APK]
+    AuditJava --> Done([Complete Analysis])
+    CompleteOutput --> Done
+```
+
+## 5. Actionable Workflows & Procedures

+```bash

-### Disassembling to Smali & Resources with Apktool:
+# Search for sensitive API endpoints, hardcoded credentials, or URLs

+```
+
+### Workflow 2: Disassembly to Smali & Resources with Apktool
 ```bash

-## 5. Verification & Safety Guidelines
-
-- **Integrity Check**: Always verify APK signature status: `apksigner verify --verbose modified_signed.apk`.
-- **Defensive Boundary**: Perform reverse engineering only on authorized applications and test binaries.
-- **Log Privacy**: Do not store decompiled proprietary source containing sensitive keys in public directories.
+## 6. Verification & Validation Steps
+- **Signature Integrity**: Verify cryptographic validity of repacked package:
+  ```bash
+  apksigner verify --verbose modified_signed.apk
+  ```
+- **Zipalign Verification**:
+  ```bash
+  zipalign -c -v 4 modified_signed.apk
+  ```
+- **Manifest Validation**: Confirm that edited permissions and activities conform to valid XML syntax without parse errors.
+
+## 7. Failure Recovery & Troubleshooting
+- **Apktool Resource Decoding Errors (`-r` flag fallback)**:
+  If a complex resource table causes `apktool d` to fail, decompile with `--no-res` to extract Smali bytecode only:
+  ```bash
+  apktool d target.apk -r -o unpacked_smali_only
+  ```
+- **Apktool Rebuild Framework Failure**:
+  Clear existing framework caches:
+  ```bash
+  rm -rf ~/.local/share/apktool/framework/
+  ```
+- **Missing Debug Keystore**:
+  Generate a new standard debug keystore if absent:
+  ```bash
+  keytool -genkey -v -keystore ~/.android/debug.keystore -storepass android -alias androiddebugkey -keypass android -keyalg RSA -keysize 2048 -validity 10000 -dname "CN=Android Debug,O=Android,C=US"
+  ```
+
+## 8. Security Boundaries & Operational Guardrails
+- **Authorized Targets Only**: Execute reverse engineering workflows only on authorized sample binaries, open-source packages, or security evaluation fixtures.
+- **Zero Credentials Persistence**: Never commit decompiled proprietary secrets, API keys, or private certificates discovered during analysis into durable repositories or memory.
+- **Isolated Sandboxing**: Perform dynamic instrumentation within isolated headless emulators or non-production test harnesses.

+Audit HTTP response headers against OWASP API security guidelines:

+- **Framing**: `X-Frame-Options: DENY`
+- **CORS**: `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` strictly configured (avoid `*` with credentials).
+

+- **Spectral Zero Errors**: Ensure `npx @stoplight/spectral-cli lint` returns exit code `0`.
+- **Response Schema Alignment**: Validate that API JSON responses strictly match declared JSON Schema response definitions.
+- **Status Codes**: Confirm error responses use appropriate 4xx/5xx status codes with standard error payload formats (`{ "error": { "code": "...", "message": "..." } }`).
+
+## 7. Failure Recovery & Troubleshooting
+- **Spectral Rule Violations**: Resolve missing `operationId`, missing 4xx/5xx responses, or unreferenced `$ref` components.
+- **CORS Preflight Failures (`OPTIONS 405/403`)**: Ensure server-side middleware handles `OPTIONS` methods and returns `Access-Control-Allow-Headers`.
+- **Schema Drift**: When backend models change, update `openapi.yaml` and regenerate types before modifying endpoint implementations.
+
+## 8. Security Boundaries & Operational Guardrails
+- **Authentication Enforcement**: Ensure all non-public endpoints enforce bearer token or API key authentication middleware.
+- **Input Sanitization**: Never interpolate unvalidated query parameters or request body parameters into raw database queries or shell commands.
+- **Zero Secrets in URLs**: Never pass API tokens, passwords, or personal identifiable information (PII) in query strings.

-Mitigation Targets:

-- **Stack Canary**: Stack protection enabled.
-- **NX**: Non-executable stack enabled.
-- **PIE**: Position Independent Executable.
-- **RPATH/RUNPATH**: No insecure library search paths.

-## 4. Symbol Extraction & Disassembly
-
+Target Mitigations Checklist:
+- **Full RELRO**: Protects Global Offset Table (`.got`) from overwrite attacks.
+- **Stack Canary**: Prevents stack buffer overflow exploitation.
+- **NX / DEP**: Disallows code execution on the stack and heap.
+- **PIE**: Position Independent Executable for full ASLR randomization.
+- **FORTIFY_SOURCE**: Replaces unsafe string/memory functions with bounds-checked variants.
+
+### Workflow 3: Symbol Extraction & Linker Analysis
 ```bash
-# Extract dynamic and exported symbols
+# Extract dynamic symbols (exported APIs and imported symbols)
 nm -D target_binary

-# Search for embedded ASCII and Unicode strings
-strings -a -t x target_binary | grep -E "https?://|api|key|secret"
-

-objdump -d -M intel --no-show-raw-insn target_binary | head -n 100
+# List shared library dependencies
+ldd target_binary || true
+readelf -d target_binary
 ```
 
-## 5. Dynamic Dependencies & Linker Inspection
-
+### Workflow 4: Disassembly & String Triage
 ```bash
-# List shared libraries required by the binary
-ldd target_binary || true
+# Disassemble .text section with Intel disassembly syntax
+objdump -d -M intel --no-show-raw-insn target_binary | head -n 120
 
-# Inspect dynamic tags
-readelf -d target_binary
+# Search for sensitive strings, API endpoints, or credentials
+strings -a -t x target_binary | grep -E "https?://|api|key|secret|token|password"
 ```
+
+## 6. Verification & Validation Steps
+- **Format Integrity**: Verify the binary header is intact and matches the expected host or target architecture.
+- **Checksec Validation**: Ensure all mitigation flags are explicitly documented in analysis logs.
+- **Symbol Cross-Reference**: Cross-reference imported libc functions (`system`, `execve`, `strcpy`, `memcpy`) to identify potential vulnerability sinks.
+
+## 7. Failure Recovery & Troubleshooting
+- **Stripped Binaries (`no symbols`)**:
+  When `nm` reports `no symbols`, inspect the dynamic symbol table (`readelf --dyn-syms`) or rely on disassembly (`objdump -d` or radare2/Ghidra).
+- **Corrupt ELF Headers**:
+  Use `hexdump -C -n 64 target_binary` to inspect raw magic bytes (`7f 45 4c 46`).
+- **Dynamic Linker Missing (`ldd fails`)**:
+  Use `readelf -l target_binary | grep interpreter` to determine the expected dynamic loader path.
+
+## 8. Security Boundaries & Operational Guardrails
+- **Static Inspection Only**: Perform analysis using static inspection tools (`readelf`, `objdump`, `strings`) without executing untrusted binaries directly.
+- **Sandbox Isolation**: If dynamic execution or debugging is required, operate exclusively inside an isolated container sandbox.
+- **No Secret Commit**: Never commit discovered binary credentials or confidential keys to repository files.

+## 6. Verification & Validation Steps

+- **User Validation**: Ensure container runs as non-root:
+  ```bash

+  ```

+
+## 7. Failure Recovery & Troubleshooting
+- **Build Context Too Large**: Check `.dockerignore` to ensure `node_modules`, `.git`, or build outputs are not sent to daemon.
+- **Permission Denied in Non-Root Container**: Ensure application directories have proper ownership (`COPY --chown=nonroot:nonroot`).
+- **Network Resolution Failure between Compose Services**: Verify services share the same Compose network and use service names as DNS hosts.
+
+## 8. Security Boundaries & Operational Guardrails
+- **Zero Secrets in Images**: Never use `ENV` or `ARG` to pass long-lived API tokens or credentials into images. Use secret mounts (`RUN --mount=type=secret`).
+- **Rootless Defaults**: Refuse to run production containers as `root` (`UID 0`).
+- **Cleanup Policy**: Always remove dangling test containers and intermediate build layers (`docker system prune -f`).

-## 5. Triage & Remediation Protocol
-
-1. **Classify Severity**: Group findings into `CRITICAL`, `HIGH`, `MEDIUM`, `LOW`, `INFORMATIONAL`.
-2. **Root-Cause Analysis**: Trace untrusted user input sources to sensitive sinks (SQL query, command execution, DOM insertion, file system access).
-3. **Remediation**:
-   - SQL Injection -> Parameterized queries / ORM prepared statements.
-   - Command Injection -> Avoid shell=True; use array-based exec.
-   - XSS -> Strict context-aware HTML escaping, Content Security Policy (CSP).
-   - Insecure Deserialization -> Safe parsers (e.g., `yaml.safe_load`, JSON).
-4. **Re-Scan**: Confirm zero remaining High/Critical findings.
+### Workflow 5: Triage & Defensive Remediation
+1. **Categorize Findings**: Group by CWE / OWASP categories.
+2. **Apply Verified Patterns**:
+   - **SQL Injection**: Parameterize queries or use typed ORM criteria.
+   - **Command Injection**: Replace `shell=True` with array-based execution arguments.
+   - **XSS**: Use context-aware auto-escaping templates and strict CSP headers.
+   - **Path Traversal**: Validate canonical paths against authorized base directories (`os.path.realpath`).
+
+## 6. Verification & Validation Steps
+- **Zero Critical / High Findings**: Verify that `semgrep scan --error` and `gitleaks detect` complete with exit code `0`.
+- **Regression Testing**: Execute test suites to ensure security fixes do not introduce functional regressions.
+- **Evidence Archival**: Save SARIF / JSON audit reports in `.environment-audit/reports/` or `docs/test_reports/`.
+
+## 7. Failure Recovery & Troubleshooting
+- **False Positive Suppression**: If a finding is verified as an intentional safe pattern, add inline nosemgrep comments (`# nosemgrep: rule-id`) with explicit rationale.
+- **Semgrep Out of Memory / Timeout**: Limit scan scope using `--exclude` flags for generated files, third-party libraries, and build artifacts.
+- **CodeQL Database Creation Failure**: Verify build command succeeds cleanly for compiled languages (Java, C/C++, Go).
+
+## 8. Security Boundaries & Operational Guardrails
+- **Zero Secrets Disclosure**: Never display or write detected plaintext secrets in conversation logs or reports. Always mask them (`sk-***`).
+- **Defensive Integrity**: Never introduce insecure workarounds or disable security linters globally to pass checks.
+- **Read-Only / Safe Fixes**: Security remediation must preserve existing application semantics and pass all verification tests.

-## 3. Vulnerability Triage & Policy
+### Workflow 5: Transitive Dependency Override Strategy
+- **npm**: Use `"overrides": { "vulnerable-pkg": "^x.y.z" }` in `package.json`.
+- **pnpm**: Use `"pnpm": { "overrides": { "vulnerable-pkg": "^x.y.z" } }`.
+- **pip**: Add pinned version to `constraints.txt`.
+- **Cargo**: Use `[patch.crates-io]` in `Cargo.toml`.
+
+## 6. Verification & Validation Steps
+- **Zero Critical/High CVEs**: Ensure `npm audit` or `trivy fs` exits with code `0`.
+- **Lockfile Consistency**: Verify lockfile is committed and clean (`git diff --stat package-lock.json`).
+- **Regression Pass**: Execute full test suite to guarantee no breaking API changes occurred.
+
+## 7. Failure Recovery & Troubleshooting
+- **Breaking API Changes in Major Version Bumps**: Do not blindly bump major versions. If a patch is not available, investigate alternative packages or apply security wrappers.
+- **Peer Dependency Conflicts (`ERESOLVE`)**: Use `--legacy-peer-deps` only as a temporary diagnostic step; resolve root conflict in manifest before shipping.
+- **Transitive Pin Refusal**: Ensure lockfile is regenerated after updating overrides (`npm install --package-lock-only`).
 
-1. **Prioritization**:
-   - `CRITICAL`: Immediate remediation required. Exploit available or remote code execution.
-   - `HIGH`: Remediate before release. Direct dependency vulnerability.
-   - `MEDIUM/LOW`: Track and update during routine dependency maintenance.
-2. **Resolution Strategy**:
-   - Upgrade direct dependency to patched minor/patch release.
-   - If transitive, use package overrides (`overrides` in `package.json`, `constraints.txt` in pip).
-   - Re-run test suite to ensure zero regressions after updates.
+## 8. Security Boundaries & Operational Guardrails
+- **Deterministic Lockfiles**: Never delete lockfiles indiscriminately; always perform controlled updates.
+- **Zero Insecure Registries**: Only fetch dependencies from verified official registries over HTTPS.
+- **Pre-Install Scripts**: Inspect third-party packages for suspicious `preinstall` or `postinstall` scripts before adding new dependencies.

+- **Mobile**: `375x812` (iPhone X/12 standard)

+- **TypeScript Clean**: Ensure `npx tsc --noEmit` exits with code `0`.

-## 5. Failure Recovery
+## 7. Failure Recovery & Troubleshooting
+- **React Hydration Mismatch**:
+  Ensure server and client render identical trees. Move browser-only APIs (`window`, `localStorage`, `navigator`) inside `useEffect` or use dynamic imports with `ssr: false`.
+- **CSS Specificity / Layer Overrides**:
+  Inspect Tailwind layer hierarchy (`@tailwind base; @tailwind components; @tailwind utilities;`) to avoid style collisions.
+- **Flaky Playwright Tests**:
+  Replace hardcoded `sleep` calls with explicit locator assertions (`await expect(locator).toBeVisible({ timeout: 5000 })`).
 
-- **Hydration Mismatch**: Inspect client/server rendering differences (e.g., date formatting, window object access during SSR).
-- **CSS Cascade / Style Overrides**: Inspect Tailwind CSS layer order (`@tailwind base; @tailwind components; @tailwind utilities;`).
+## 8. Security Boundaries & Operational Guardrails
+- **XSS Prevention**: Never use `dangerouslySetInnerHTML` with unsanitized user-generated markup. Always use DOMPurify if HTML rendering is required.
+- **No Sensitive Data in Web Storage**: Never persist authentication tokens, passwords, or encryption keys in `localStorage` or `sessionStorage`. Use `httpOnly` secure cookies.
+- **Isolated Browser Contexts**: Browser test automation must execute in isolated guest profiles without loading personal user profiles or cookies.

+## 7. Failure Recovery & Troubleshooting
+- **API Version Deprecation (`apiVersion: extensions/v1beta1`)**:
+  Update to current stable APIs (`apps/v1` for Deployments, `networking.k8s.io/v1` for Ingress).
+- **Missing Custom Resource Definitions (CRDs)**:
+  Use `kubeconform -ignore-missing-schemas` when validating third-party CRDs without local schemas.
+- **Helm Template Variable Nil Pointer**:

+
+## 8. Security Boundaries & Operational Guardrails
+- **Restricted Pod Security**: Reject privileged containers and root users (`runAsUser: 0`).
+- **Read-Only Root Filesystems**: Require ephemeral volumes (`emptyDir: {}`) for temporary files.
+- **Zero Secrets in Plaintext**: Never commit raw unencrypted `Secret` manifests into git. Use SealedSecrets, External Secrets Operator, or Vault references.

+## 1. Purpose & Scope
+This skill cleanly concludes an Antigravity working session, distills acquired insights, records a timestamped episodic session log, compacts `CURRENT.md` to under 30 lines, scans candidate memory for secrets, and classifies candidate durable memories across the 9 knowledge types and 0–5 durability scale.
 
-## Overview
-`/end-session` conducts a retrospective on the active session, generates a timestamped markdown log under `.agents/memory/sessions/YYYY-MM-DD-HHMM.md`, compacts `.agents/memory/CURRENT.md` to under 30 lines, extracts candidate durable memories with 9-type classification and 0–5 durability scores, and ensures zero secrets are leaked.
+## 2. Activation Triggers
+Activate this skill when:
+- Executing the `/end-session` command.
+- Concluding an engineering milestone or active work turn.
+- Persisting session context and handoff state before switching tasks or disconnecting.

+```

+- `4`: Domain/Tool memory (`~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/memory/domains/` or `tools/`).

-## 2. Memory Classification Framework (Phase 10)
-
-Every memory candidate evaluated by `/promote-memory` MUST be classified into exactly one **Primary Type** and assigned a verified **Durability Score (0–5)**.
-
-### 2.1 The 9 Knowledge Types
-
-| Type | Name | Definition & Scope | Example Target Destination |
-| :--- | :--- | :--- | :--- |
-| `EPHEMERAL` | Ephemeral Noise | Temporary calculations, intermediate scratch output, transient debug traces, single-command outputs. | **Discarded immediately** (No storage) |
-| `SESSION` | Session Context | Working state relevant only within the active session (e.g. current in-progress task status, uncommitted state). | Active Session Log (`sessions/`) |
-| `PROJECT` | Project Knowledge | Repository-specific architectural patterns, component structures, internal APIs, coding standards, repo conventions. | `.agents/memory/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `CURRENT.md` |
-| `DOMAIN` | Domain Knowledge | Reusable concepts and patterns applicable across multiple projects within a tech domain (e.g., React, Go, Rust, K8s). | `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/memory/domains/<domain>.md` |
-| `TOOL` | Tooling & CLI | Operational knowledge, flags, edge cases, workarounds, and quirks for CLI tools, runtimes, linters, or SDKs. | `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/memory/tools/<tool>.md` |
-| `GLOBAL` | Global Universal | Universal developer conventions, immutable environment constraints, global user preferences. | `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/memory/GENERAL.md`, `USER.md`, `ENVIRONMENT.md` |
-| `DECISION` | Architectural Decision | Formal technical decisions recording choice, alternatives evaluated, context, rationale, and timestamp. | `.agents/memory/DECISIONS.md` |
-| `GOTCHA` | Non-Obvious Trap / Bug | Subtle bugs, undocumented behavior, dependency edge cases, performance traps, and verified workarounds. | `.agents/memory/GOTCHAS.md` or `memory/tools/<tool>.md` |
-| `PROCEDURE` | Operational Procedure | Step-by-step repeatable recipes, build/deploy instructions, migration checklists, verification playbooks. | `.agents/memory/PROCEDURES.md` or `memory/domains/` |
-

-### 2.2 Durability Scale (0 to 5) & Promotion Policy
+# Workspace Skill: Memory Promotion & Classification (`/promote-memory`)
+
+## 1. Purpose & Scope
+This skill governs the systematic evaluation, classification, security verification, contradiction detection, and promotion of candidate memory items into durable workspace or global memory tiers. It enforces strict empirical evidence standards, a 0–5 durability scale, and a 9-type classification taxonomy.
+
+## 2. Activation Triggers
+Activate this skill when:
+- Executing the `/promote-memory` command.
+- An architectural decision is finalized (`.agents/memory/DECISIONS.md`).
+- A non-obvious bug, trap, or gotcha with a verified workaround is discovered (`.agents/memory/GOTCHAS.md`).
+- Reusable domain or tool patterns are established (`~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/memory/domains/` or `tools/`).
+- Promoting end-of-session candidate memories to persistent storage.
+
+## 3. Prerequisites & Environment Verification
+Verify presence of memory structures and security scanners:
+```bash
+# Verify directory structure
+test -d .agents/memory || mkdir -p .agents/memory
+python3 --version
+```
 
+## 4. Decision Tree & Workflow Routing
 ```mermaid
-graph TD
-    Candidate[Memory Candidate] --> SecretCheck{Contains Secrets?}
-    SecretCheck -- Yes --> Reject[REJECT: Security Violation]
+flowchart TD
+    Start([Memory Candidate]) --> SecretCheck{Contains Secrets?}
+    SecretCheck -- Yes --> Reject[REJECT: Security Violation & Mask]
     SecretCheck -- No --> EvidenceCheck{Verified Evidence?}
+    
     EvidenceCheck -- No / Speculative --> Discard01[Score 0-1: DISCARD]
     EvidenceCheck -- Yes --> ScoreScale{Evaluate Durability}
+    
     ScoreScale -- "Score 0-1" --> Discard01
     ScoreScale -- "Score 2" --> SessionLog[Score 2: Session Log Only]
     ScoreScale -- "Score 3" --> ProjectMem[Score 3: Project Memory .agents/memory/]
     ScoreScale -- "Score 4" --> DomainToolMem[Score 4: Domain/Tool Memory ~/.gemini/.../domains|tools/]
     ScoreScale -- "Score 5" --> GlobalMem[Score 5: Global Memory ~/.gemini/.../memory/]
+    
+    ProjectMem --> ContraCheck[Check Contradictions & Superseded]
+    DomainToolMem --> ContraCheck
+    GlobalMem --> ContraCheck
+    
+    ContraCheck --> WriteMem[Format Entry & Write to Target File]
+    WriteMem --> SyncIndex[Update Relevant INDEX.md]
+    SyncIndex --> Done([Complete Promotion])
+    Discard01 --> Done
+    SessionLog --> Done
+    Reject --> Done
 ```
 
-#### Durability Rubric:
-- **Level 0 (Discard)**: Instantaneous throwaway debug outputs, syntax error corrections during normal typing, raw terminal noise.
-  - *Action*: Discard immediately.
-- **Level 1 (Discard / Reject)**: Speculative theories, unconfirmed bug root causes, subjective guesses, unverified preferences.
-  - *Action*: Discard immediately. Never persist unverified speculation.
-- **Level 2 (Session Only)**: Task progress, pending steps for current task, temporary working file paths, subagent coordination states.
-  - *Action*: Retain only in current session log / context buffer; do not promote to durable storage.
-- **Level 3 (Project Memory)**: Project-specific design decisions, repo architecture, repo gotchas, custom build steps, internal library contracts.
-  - *Action*: Promote to Workspace Project Memory (`.agents/memory/`).
-- **Level 4 (Domain / Tool Memory)**: Framework-wide gotchas (e.g. Next.js 14 App Router quirks), tool nuances (e.g. git worktree edge-cases, Docker buildx flags), repeatable tech-stack procedures.
-  - *Action*: Promote to Global Domain or Tool Memory (`~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/memory/domains/` or `tools/`).
-- **Level 5 (Global Memory)**: Fundamental user directives, global toolchain facts, universal developer workflows applicable across all repositories.
-  - *Action*: Promote to Global Memory (`~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/memory/` -> `GENERAL.md`, `USER.md`, `ENVIRONMENT.md`).
-
----
+## 5. Actionable Workflows & Procedures
+
+### Workflow 1: The 9 Knowledge Types Taxonomy
+Every candidate item must be classified into exactly one type:
+1. `EPHEMERAL`: Transient debug output, calculations -> Discard immediately.
+2. `SESSION`: Active task status -> Session log only.
+3. `PROJECT`: Repository-specific architecture, patterns -> `.agents/memory/ARCHITECTURE.md`.
+4. `DOMAIN`: Reusable tech-domain knowledge (React, K8s, Go) -> `memory/domains/<domain>.md`.
+5. `TOOL`: CLI operational quirks, flags -> `memory/tools/<tool>.md`.
+6. `GLOBAL`: Universal developer conventions, preferences -> `memory/GENERAL.md`, `USER.md`, `ENVIRONMENT.md`.
+7. `DECISION`: Formal architectural choices -> `.agents/memory/DECISIONS.md`.
+8. `GOTCHA`: Non-obvious bugs and verified fixes -> `.agents/memory/GOTCHAS.md`.
+9. `PROCEDURE`: Repeatable step-by-step playbooks -> `.agents/memory/PROCEDURES.md`.
+
+### Workflow 2: Secret Scanning & Truth Verification
+1. **Secret Scan**: Inspect candidate text against credential patterns (API keys, private keys, auth headers).
+2. **Truth Criteria**: Candidate must be:
+   - Empirically observed and verified in live execution.
+   - Or explicitly directed by the user.
+   - Free from speculative hedging words ("maybe", "probably", "might be").
+
+### Workflow 3: Contradiction Check & Superseded History
+Before appending to a durable memory file:
+1. Search target file for related historical assertions.
+2. If contradicted by live codebase reality, preserve historical context via `## Superseded`:
+```markdown
+### Superseded Entry
+- **Previous**: [Old Fact / Decision]
+- **Current**: [New Fact / Decision]
+- **Reason**: [Why the change occurred]
+- **Date**: YYYY-MM-DD
+```

-
-## 4. Edge Cases & Safety Guardrails
 
-- **Ambiguous Scope**: If uncertain between Project (3) and Global (5), default to **Project (3)** to prevent cross-contamination.
-- **Speculative Bug Fixes**: If a bug fix was applied but not yet confirmed by automated tests or verified runtime execution, assign Durability `2` (Session Only) until validated.
-- **User vs Inferred**: Inferred user habits get Durability `1` (discarded). Explicit user commands ("Always use pnpm in this repo") get Durability `3` (Project) or `5` (Global User preference).
+## 6. Verification & Validation Steps
+- **Target File Integrity**: Verify formatted block was appended cleanly to the correct markdown file.
+- **Index Accuracy**: Confirm `INDEX.md` contains an accurate entry pointing to the modified file.
+- **Secret Scanner Clean**: Confirm no credentials leaked into the target file.
+
+## 7. Failure Recovery & Troubleshooting
+- **Ambiguous Scope (Project vs Global)**:
+  Always default to **Project Memory (Score 3)** to prevent workspace-specific contamination in global tiers.
+- **Unverified Bug Workaround**:
+  Assign score `2` (Session Only) until the workaround is confirmed by automated tests.
+- **Contradiction Detected**:
+  Do not silently overwrite previous entries. Always format as `## Superseded` to preserve historical lineage.
+
+## 8. Security Boundaries & Operational Guardrails
+- **Zero Secrets**: Absolute prohibition against writing credentials, tokens, or private keys.
+- **Authoritative Repo State**: Live code in the repository strictly outranks memory records.
+- **User Intent Supremacy**: Explicit user instructions outrank inferred preferences.

+# Workspace Skill: Memory Reorganization (`/reorganize-memory`)
+
+## 1. Purpose & Scope
+This skill provides structured procedures to clean, deduplicate, merge, compact, and partition persistent memory files across workspace (`.agents/memory/`) and global (`~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/memory/`) tiers, while maintaining strict context line budgets and index synchronization.
+
+## 2. Activation Triggers
+Activate this skill when:
+- Executing `/reorganize-memory`.
+- Memory files exceed recommended line budgets (`CURRENT.md` > 30 lines, durable files > 250 lines).
+- Duplicate assertions or fragmented notes are identified during memory audits.
+- Archiving older session logs into quarterly bundles or consolidating topic notes.
+
+## 3. Prerequisites & Environment Verification

+```bash

+test -d .agents/memory || mkdir -p .agents/memory
+```
+

+```mermaid

+

+    ConsolidateDups --> CheckContra[3. Scan for Contradictory Records]
+

+

+    SplitFiles --> SyncIndexes
+

+    FixIssues --> RunAudit
+    PassAudit -- Yes --> GenReport[7. Output Reorganization Summary]

+```

+## 6. Verification & Validation Steps
+- **CTest Zero Failures**: Ensure `ctest` completes with `100% tests passed`.
+- **Zero Sanitizer Reports**: Verify no `AddressSanitizer: heap-buffer-overflow` or `use-after-free` warnings during execution.
+- **Valgrind Clean**: Confirm `0 errors from 0 contexts` and `all heap blocks were freed`.
+
+## 7. Failure Recovery & Troubleshooting
+- **Undefined Reference Linker Error (`ld: undefined reference to 'foo'`)**:
+  Check `target_link_libraries` in `CMakeLists.txt` to ensure implementation libraries are linked.
+- **ASan Stack Buffer Overflow**:
+  Inspect array index calculations and bounds checks at the source line reported by ASan.
+- **Missing CMake Dependency**:
+  Install required dev packages via `apt-get` or check `find_package()` names.
+
+## 8. Security Boundaries & Operational Guardrails
+- **Bounds Checking**: Enforce bounds checks on all buffers; disallow unsafe functions (`gets`, `strcpy`, `sprintf`).
+- **Compiler Warnings as Errors**: Maintain `-Wall -Wextra -Werror` to catch uninitialized variables and type mismatches early.
+- **Safe Sandboxing**: Run untrusted native test executions inside container boundaries.

-## 3. Evidence Generation & Certification
-
-1. Record execution logs with timestamps and exit codes.
-2. Produce structured markdown test reports under `docs/test_reports/`.
-3. Certify that all non-optional components pass the Definition of Done.
+   ```bash
+   apktool -version && jadx --version && apksigner --version
+   ```
+4. **WebAssembly**:
+   ```bash
+   wasm-validate fixtures/wasm/sample.wasm && wasmtime fixtures/wasm/sample.wasm

+   ```
+6. **Network Forensics**:
+   ```bash

+   ```
+
+### Workflow 3: Cryptographic Evidence Collection & Reporting
+1. Compute SHA-256 hashes of test fixtures and generated binary outputs:
+   ```bash
+   sha256sum fixtures/**/* | head -n 30
+   ```
+2. Save structured markdown test reports under `docs/test_reports/YYYY-MM-DD-test-report.md`.
+
+## 6. Verification & Validation Steps
+- **Definition of Done Compliance**:
+  - Code compiles without warnings/errors.
+  - All test suites exit with code `0`.
+  - Zero Critical/High vulnerabilities reported by SAST/secret scanners.
+  - Live execution evidence recorded with timestamps and command outputs.
+
+## 7. Failure Recovery & Troubleshooting
+- **Toolchain Gap / Missing Binary**:
+  Inspect `agy-env-doctor` output and install required packages via `apt-get` or package managers.
+- **Partial Test Failure**:
+  Isolate failing test domain, inspect stderr logs, and apply targeted fixes before re-running the full suite.
+- **Flaky Playwright Test**:
+  Verify display server / headless browser flags (`--headless --no-sandbox`).
+
+## 8. Security Boundaries & Operational Guardrails
+- **Read-Only Verification**: Test verification scripts must not modify production configurations or delete persistent data.
+- **Zero Secrets in Test Evidence**: Ensure test reports do not log environment variables containing tokens or private keys.
+- **Hermetic Test Fixtures**: Test runs must rely exclusively on self-contained local fixtures.

+
+## 6. Verification & Validation Steps
+- **Validation Exit Code**: Ensure `wasm-validate module.wasm` returns exit code `0`.
+- **Export Verification**: Verify all intended function exports are present in `wasm-objdump -x`.
+- **Runtime Execution**: Confirm `wasmtime` or Node.js executes without traps (`unreachable`, `out of bounds memory access`).
+
+## 7. Failure Recovery & Troubleshooting
+- **Type Mismatch During Validation (`type mismatch: expected i32, got f32`)**:
+  Disassemble to `.wat` via `wasm2wat` and inspect stack parameter types for the failing instruction.
+- **Unreachable Trap Encountered**:
+  Check division by zero, null pointer dereferences, or explicit `unreachable` opcodes in decompiled WAT.
+- **Linear Memory Out of Bounds**:
+  Ensure module memory size is properly declared or allow memory growth (`(memory 1 10)`).
+
+## 8. Security Boundaries & Operational Guardrails
+- **Linear Memory Sandboxing**: Ensure WASM modules cannot read or write outside their allocated linear memory boundaries.
+- **Restricted Host Imports**: Audit imported host functions to prevent untrusted WASM code from invoking arbitrary system calls.
+- **Determinism**: Disallow non-deterministic floating point NaN bit patterns in security-critical routines.

-## 6. Antigravity Lifecycle Hooks
+## 6. Tool Selection Policy & Hierarchy
+
+When selecting tools, follow the efficiency hierarchy—**never use the heaviest tool by default**:
+- **Need current library documentation?** → `Context7` MCP (`query-docs`)
+- **Need browser interaction?** → `playwright` MCP / CLI
+- **Need GitHub repository data?** → `github` MCP / `gh` CLI
+- **Need source code security review?** → `semgrep` (AST matching) → `codeql` (deep taint tracking)
+- **Need dependency/container security?** → `gitleaks` (secrets) → `trivy` / `osv-scanner` / `pip-audit`
+- **Need Android APK analysis?** → `apktool` (resources/manifest) + `jadx` (Java source) + `apksigner`
+- **Need binary triage?** → `file` + `checksec` + binutils (`readelf`, `nm`, `strings`)
+- **Need deep binary analysis?** → `r2` / `rizin` (fast CLI) → `Ghidra Headless` (`analyzeHeadless`) / `hexana`
+- **Need PCAP analysis?** → `capinfos` (metadata) → `tshark` (filter/extract) → `scapy` (synthesis)
+- **Need Android build?** → Gradle wrapper (`./gradlew`) + Android SDK + `adb`
+
+---
+
+## 7. Antigravity Lifecycle Hooks
 
 Hooks are declared in `.agents/hooks.json` and executed deterministically:
-1. **`PreInvocation`**: Context reminder and memory state validation.
-2. **`PreToolUse`**: Security gatekeeper intercepting `write_to_file`, `replace_file_content`, and `run_command` to block secret leaks.
-3. **`PostToolUse`**: Syntax tracking, lint feedback recording, and tool execution error tracking.
-4. **`Stop`**: Session guard verifying idle state and background tasks.
+1. **`PreInvocation` (`scripts/hooks/pre_invocation.py`)**: Context reminder and memory state validation.
+2. **`PreToolUse` (`scripts/hooks/pre_tool_use.py`)**: Security gatekeeper intercepting `write_to_file`, `replace_file_content`, and `run_command` to block secret leaks.
+3. **`PostToolUse` (`scripts/hooks/post_tool_use.py`)**: Syntax tracking, lint feedback recording, and tool execution error tracking.
+4. **`Stop` (`scripts/hooks/stop_guard.py`)**: Session guard verifying idle state and background tasks.

+## 9. Antigravity 8-Tier Memory Operating System
+
+The workstation enforces an 8-tier decoupled Memory OS governed by [`Instruction.md`](file:///workspaces/codespaces-blank/Instruction.md) and [`SPEC.md`](file:///workspaces/codespaces-blank/.agents/memory-upgrade/SPEC.md):
+1. **Tier 1 (Working Memory)**: `.agents/memory/CURRENT.md` (bounded $< 30$ lines).
+2. **Tier 2 (Episodic Memory)**: `.agents/memory/sessions/YYYY-MM-DD-HHMM.md` (chronological session journals).
+3. **Tier 3 (Project Knowledge)**: `.agents/memory/{DECISIONS,GOTCHAS,ARCHITECTURE}.md`.
+4. **Tier 4 (Global Durable)**: `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/memory/` (`GENERAL.md`, `USER.md`, `POLICY.md`, `tools/`, `domains/`).
+5. **Tier 5 (Semantic Retrieval Substrate)**: Derived vector index in `.agents/memory/.memsearch/` & Milvus Lite (`~/.memsearch/milvus.db`).
+6. **Tier 6 (Candidate Experience Bus)**: Staged inbox at `.agents/memory/inbox/candidates.jsonl`.
+7. **Tier 7 (Procedural Memory)**: Distilled workflows staged at `.agents/memory/inbox/procedures/`.
+8. **Tier 8 (Feedback & Telemetry Ledger)**: Retrieval metrics at `.agents/memory/retrieval_ledger.jsonl`.
+

-## 3. Remediation & Workaround Log
-
-### 3.1 Android Emulator Fallback Policy
-- **Observed State:** `/dev/kvm` does not exist or is not readable by non-root user within Azure container.
-- **Remediation Implemented:**
-  1. `adb` and `sdkmanager` configured with full headless platform-tools.
-  2. Gradle automated test harness configured for JVM unit tests and Robolectric headless execution.
-  3. CI pipeline template created for hardware-accelerated device farms.
-
-### 3.2 SRE Headless Automation
-- **Observed State:** Interactive GUI applications cannot render directly into terminal web sessions.
-- **Remediation Implemented:**
-  1. Configured Ghidra Headless Analyzer in `/usr/local/ghidra/support/analyzeHeadless`.
-  2. Created automated script wrapper `tools/scripts/ghidra-analyze.sh` which provisions temporary projects, imports ELF/PE/DEX binaries, executes decompiler analysis, and exports decompiled C representations.
-  3. Integrated Radare2 (`r2`) and Rizin (`rizin`) for instant CLI terminal disassembly.
-
-### 3.3 Network Forensic Guardrails
-- **Observed State:** Running `tcpdump -i eth0` in non-root container generates permission errors.
-- **Remediation Implemented:**
-  1. Provided synthetic PCAP generator (`fixtures/network/generate_sample_pcap.py`) powered by Scapy.
-  2. Configured TShark, Capinfos, and Tcpdump for high-speed offline packet dissection, protocol filtering, and HTTP payload extraction (`tools/scripts/pcap-http-report.sh`).

+## 2. Platform Constraint Remediation & Fallback Strategies
+
+### 2.1 Hardware Virtualization (KVM) Constraint
+- **Constraint**: Cloud-hosted Azure VMs do not expose `/dev/kvm` to nested Docker containers.
+- **Impact**: Full Android QEMU system emulators cannot run with hardware acceleration.
+- **Engineered Fallback**:
+  1. Headless JVM unit tests running via Robolectric mock SDK environments.
+  2. Gradle automated test harness executing in headless CI mode (`./gradlew testDebugUnitTest --no-daemon`).
+  3. ADB over TCP/IP bridging allowing connection to remote physical devices or external cloud device farms.
+
+### 2.2 Cloud Headless Environment (GUI) Constraint
+- **Constraint**: The Codespaces environment is accessed via browser/remote terminal without an active X11/Wayland display server.
+- **Impact**: Desktop GUI applications (e.g. interactive Ghidra GUI window, desktop browser windows) cannot render visually.
+- **Engineered Fallback**:
+  1. **Ghidra**: Fully scripted via `$GHIDRA_HOME/support/analyzeHeadless` and `tools/scripts/ghidra-analyze.sh`.
+  2. **Playwright**: Configured with `--headless=new`, `--no-sandbox`, and `--disable-dev-shm-usage`. Full visual verification via automated PNG screenshot generation and DOM accessibility snapshots.
+  3. **Disassembly**: Interactive terminal workflows handled via Radare2 (`r2 -q`) and Rizin (`rizin`).
+
+### 2.3 Operating System Boundary (macOS / iOS) Constraint
+- **Constraint**: Apple Xcode, iOS SDKs, and CoreSimulator require macOS Darwin kernels and cannot be installed on Linux.
+- **Impact**: Direct local compilation of `.ipa` packages and iOS simulator execution is unsupported on Linux.
+- **Engineered Fallback**:
+  1. Linux Swift runtime and SourceKit-LSP configured for Swift code authoring and syntax validation.
+  2. Formal cross-platform handoff architecture documented in `docs/ios-macos-handoff.md`.
+  3. Validated JSON schema in `docs/schemas/ios-handoff.schema.json` and sample payload in `docs/schemas/sample-handoff.json` for deterministic CI/CD job dispatch to macOS runners.
+
+### 2.4 Container Privilege & Network Sniffing Constraint
+- **Constraint**: Non-root container users cannot put network interfaces into promiscuous capture mode without `CAP_NET_RAW` privileges.
+- **Impact**: Running `tcpdump -i eth0` generates permission errors.
+- **Engineered Fallback**:
+  1. Dissection workflows decoupled from live packet capture.
+  2. Full offline PCAP dissection via `tshark -r`, `capinfos`, `editcap`, and `mergecap`.
+  3. Python `scapy` library configured for deterministic synthetic traffic creation and protocol fuzzing (`fixtures/network/generate_sample_pcap.py`).

 const { chromium } = require('playwright');
 const path = require('path');

+const http = require('http');

+const PORT = process.env.TEST_PORT || process.env.PORT || 3000;
+const HOST = process.env.HOST || '127.0.0.1';
+const targetUrl = process.env.BASE_URL || `http://${HOST}:${PORT}/`;
+
+function checkServerReachable(url, timeoutMs = 3000) {
+  return new Promise((resolve) => {
+    try {
+      const u = new URL(url);
+      const req = http.get(
+        {
+          hostname: u.hostname,
+          port: u.port,
+          path: '/health',
+          timeout: timeoutMs,
+        },
+        (res) => {
+          resolve(res.statusCode === 200);
+        }
+      );
+      req.on('error', () => resolve(false));
+      req.on('timeout', () => {
+        req.destroy();
+        resolve(false);
+      });
+    } catch {
+      resolve(false);
+    }
+  });

-# ANTIGRAVITY CODESPACE — AUTONOMOUS ENGINEERING WORKSTATION BOOTSTRAP
-You are operating inside a GitHub Codespace on a Linux development container. Your 
-mission is to transform the current Codespace into a **reproducible, professional, 
-multi-domain agentic software engineering workstation** for: * Android application 
-development * iOS/Swift development preparation and macOS handoff * Web 
-application development * Frontend development * Backend/API development * 
-Database development * Native/C/C++ development * WebAssembly development * 
-Application binary analysis and manipulation * Android reverse engineering * 
-Software reverse engineering * Defensive cybersecurity and secure-code analysis * 
-Network analysis and packet forensics * Containerized development * 
-Kubernetes/cloud-native development * AI-assisted testing and verification You are 
-not merely installing editor extensions. You are building an **agent-operable 
-engineering environment** in which the IDE, CLI tools, MCP servers, Skills, hooks, 
-analyzers, test infrastructure, and development-container configuration cooperate. 
----
-# 1. OPERATING MODE
-Operate as a senior DevOps engineer, systems engineer, application-security 
-engineer, reverse engineer, Android engineer, web engineer and AI-agent 
-infrastructure engineer. Be autonomous. Do not wait for me after every individual 
-installation step. Do not ask unnecessary questions. Make reasonable engineering 
-decisions yourself. Ask me only when a genuine blocker requires information or 
-authorization that cannot be inferred safely. Do not claim something is installed 
-unless you have actually verified it. Do not claim something works unless you have 
-executed an appropriate verification. Do not silently replace failed tools with 
-unrelated tools. When a requested tool is incompatible with this Linux Codespace, 
-clearly classify it as: * `AVAILABLE` * `PARTIAL` * 
-`UNAVAILABLE_ON_CURRENT_PLATFORM` * `OPTIONAL` * `FAILED_REQUIRES_REMEDIATION` 
-Then provide the best supported alternative. ---
-# 2. NON-NEGOTIABLE EXECUTION LOOP
-For every major subsystem use this loop: ```text DISCOVER ↓ CHECK CURRENT STATE ↓ 
-PLAN
-    ↓ INSTALL / CONFIGURE ↓ VERIFY ↓ INTEGRATE WITH AGENT ↓ RUN FUNCTIONAL TEST ↓ 
-REMEDIATE FAILURES
-    ↓ VERIFY AGAIN ↓ RECORD RESULT ``` Never use: ```text install everything 
-assume success finish ``` Instead use: ```text install → inspect output → verify 
-binary/version/config → test actual capability → repair → retest ``` ---
-# 3. FIRST ACTION: FORENSIC ENVIRONMENT INVENTORY
-Before changing anything, inspect the environment. Determine: ```bash uname -a 
-uname -m cat /etc/os-release id pwd df -h free -h nproc git --version gh --version 
-code --version node --version npm --version python3 --version java -version gcc 
---version clang --version cmake --version docker --version ``` Also inspect: 
-```bash command -v agy agy --version agy models ``` Then inspect: * existing 
-`.devcontainer/` * existing `.vscode/` * existing `.agents/` * existing 
-`AGENTS.md` * existing `GEMINI.md` * existing project build systems * existing 
-package managers * existing Android configuration * existing Gradle wrapper * 
-existing Node configuration * existing Python configuration * existing Docker 
-configuration * existing Kubernetes configuration * existing CI configuration * 
-current VS Code extensions * current MCP configuration * current Antigravity 
-plugins/skills * current PATH * current environment variables relevant to 
-development tooling Do not overwrite existing project configuration blindly. 
-Create: ```text .environment-baseline/ ``` with a machine/tool inventory 
-describing what already exists. ---
-# 4. REPOSITORY SAFETY
-Before modifying repository configuration: ```bash git status --short git branch 
---show-current git log -5 --oneline ``` Determine whether the working tree 
-contains user changes. Never destroy or overwrite unrelated user work. Never run 
-destructive commands such as: ```bash rm -rf git reset --hard git clean -fd git 
-push --force ``` unless explicitly required by a later user instruction. Do not 
-commit secrets. Do not place API keys, access tokens, passwords, private 
-certificates, SSH keys or credentials into: * source code * `devcontainer.json` * 
-`.vscode/settings.json` * MCP configuration tracked by Git * `AGENTS.md` * Skills 
-* shell scripts * CI files Use Codespaces secrets / environment variables / secure 
-credential mechanisms where appropriate. ---
-# 5. ARCHITECTURE TO BUILD
-Create or improve the following structure where appropriate: ```text 
-.devcontainer/
-    devcontainer.json Dockerfile scripts/ bootstrap.sh verify-toolchain.sh 
-        verify-security-stack.sh verify-android.sh verify-binary-stack.sh 
-        verify-network-stack.sh verify-mcp.sh
-.vscode/ settings.json tasks.json extensions.json .agents/ mcp_config.json skills/ 
-        android-engineering/ android-reverse-engineering/ web-engineering/ 
-        frontend-engineering/ backend-engineering/ api-engineering/ 
-        binary-analysis/ reverse-engineering/ native-development/ 
-        wasm-engineering/ cybersecurity-review/ network-analysis/ 
-        container-engineering/ kubernetes-engineering/ verification/ 
-        dependency-audit/
-    agents/ environment-auditor/ android-engineer/ web-engineer/ backend-engineer/ 
-        binary-analyst/ security-auditor/ network-analyst/ verification-engineer/ 
-        adversarial-reviewer/
-    hooks.json AGENTS.md tools/ scripts/ reports/ docs/ engineering-environment.md 
-    toolchain-matrix.md compatibility-matrix.md
-``` Do not create unnecessary files if existing repository architecture already 
-provides an equivalent structure. Prefer integration over duplication. ---
-# 6. VS CODE EXTENSIONS
-Install and configure the most relevant extensions from the following set.
-## Core agent / environment
-Install: ```text Google.google-antigravity ``` If the official Antigravity 
-extension is unavailable in the current Codespaces environment, do not substitute 
-an unofficial extension unless there is a concrete compatibility reason. ---
-## Code correctness / quality
-Install: ```text dbaeumer.vscode-eslint esbenp.prettier-vscode 
-SonarSource.sonarlint-vscode Semgrep.semgrep GitHub.vscode-codeql ``` Use 
-project-local versions where applicable. Do not create conflicting 
-formatter/linter configurations. ---
-## Containers / infrastructure
-Install: ```text ms-azuretools.vscode-docker 
-ms-kubernetes-tools.vscode-kubernetes-tools github.vscode-github-actions 
-github.vscode-pull-request-github ``` Verify each extension is compatible with 
-remote Codespaces execution before relying upon it. ---
-## Web/frontend
-Install where relevant: ```text bradlc.vscode-tailwindcss ``` If the repository 
-uses React/Next/Vue/Svelte/Angular-specific tooling, inspect the actual project 
-before adding framework-specific extensions. Do not install random extension packs 
-merely because they contain popular extensions. Prefer first-party or mature 
-extensions. ---
-## Android

-AndroidGradleTools.android-ide-extension levkosyk.vscode-android-tools 
-Surendrajat.apklab ``` For APKLab specifically, verify that its dependencies and 
-Android reverse-engineering workflow operate correctly in this Linux Codespace. 
----
-## Binary analysis
-Evaluate: ```text JetBrains.hexana-wasm ``` Do not assume that installing the 
-extension automatically exposes its capabilities to Antigravity CLI. Determine 
-whether its MCP server can be consumed directly by Antigravity. If not, document 
-the limitation and integrate equivalent command-line functionality instead. ---
-## iOS / Swift
-Evaluate: ```text sweetpad.sweetpad ``` Because the current environment is 
-Linux/Codespaces: * install only if the extension can usefully operate in this 
-environment * do not fake iOS simulator/device functionality * do not claim Xcode 
-is available * do not claim native iOS builds work Prepare a macOS handoff 
-configuration where practical. Include: ```text Swift SourceKit-LSP swift-format 
-SwiftLint xcodebuild xcode-build-server SweetPad ``` as the macOS-side intended 
-toolchain. Document which parts can execute in Linux and which require 
-macOS/Xcode. ---

-yarn.lock bun.lock ``` and preserve the project's existing package manager. For 
-Python repositories inspect: ```text pyproject.toml requirements.txt uv.lock 
-poetry.lock Pipfile ``` before choosing installation tooling. ---
-# 9. ANDROID TOOLCHAIN
-Install a reproducible headless Android development environment. Use the current 
-Android command-line tools model. Set: ```text ANDROID_HOME PATH ``` 
-appropriately. Use the latest stable command-line tools unless the project 
-requires a pinned version. Install project-appropriate: ```text platform-tools 
-build-tools platforms cmdline-tools NDK CMake emulator system images ``` Do not 
-install arbitrary Android API levels unnecessarily. Inspect the repository first: 
-```text compileSdk targetSdk minSdk buildToolsVersion ndkVersion AGP version 
-Kotlin version Gradle version ``` Choose versions compatible with the actual 
-project. Use: ```bash sdkmanager --list sdkmanager --licenses sdkmanager ... ``` 
-for headless installation. Verify: ```bash adb version sdkmanager --version 
-avdmanager --version emulator -version ``` For projects that require emulator 
-testing: 1. Determine whether the Codespace supports nested virtualization/KVM. 2. 
-Determine whether graphical emulation is possible. 3. Prefer headless emulator 
-mode where supported. 4. Do not claim emulator functionality if hardware 
-acceleration or required host capabilities are absent. Create an optional reusable 
-AVD only when practical. Where emulator execution is impossible, create a 
-documented external-device / CI fallback. ---

-APPROVAL REQUIRED ``` by default. ---

-reverse-engineering native-development wasm-engineering cybersecurity-review

-verification ``` Each Skill must: * have concise frontmatter * clearly describe 
-when it should activate * contain decision logic * define prerequisites * define 
-allowed tools * define verification * define failure recovery * avoid duplicating

-every Skill. ---

-Purpose: ```text run tests run builds run static analysis produce evidence ```
-## adversarial-reviewer
-Purpose: ```text independently review the implementation search for regressions

-contexts and return concise evidence-focused results. ---
-# 29. ANTIGRAVITY HOOKS
-Create hooks that improve reliability without becoming dangerous or annoying. 
-Useful hooks include: ```text PreToolUse PostToolUse Stop ``` Potential behaviors: 
-* detect modifications outside the workspace * warn before destructive commands * 
-run lightweight validation after relevant edits * capture important command 
-failures * run formatting after code generation where appropriate * prevent 
-accidental secret commits * verify repository cleanliness before major operations 
-* record installation state * verify that tools reported as installed actually 
-exist Never create a hook that blindly executes arbitrary commands from 
-model-generated input. ---
-# 30. AGENTS.md

-concise enough to remain useful. Do not duplicate every Skill in AGENTS.md. ---
-# 31. DEFINITION OF DONE
-The environment is NOT complete merely because packages installed. The environment 
-is complete only after you have verified as many of the following as the platform 
-permits: ```text [ ] Antigravity CLI available [ ] Antigravity model listing works 
-[ ] VS Code integration works [ ] MCP configuration parses [ ] Context7 reachable 
-[ ] GitHub tooling available [ ] Playwright launches [ ] Node works [ ] Python 
-works [ ] C/C++ works [ ] Java works [ ] Gradle works [ ] Android SDK works [ ] 
-adb works [ ] APK tooling works [ ] JADX works [ ] APKLab dependencies work [ ] 
-Ghidra headless works [ ] radare2 works [ ] rizin works [ ] GDB works [ ] LLDB 
-works [ ] WebAssembly tools work [ ] Semgrep works [ ] CodeQL works [ ] Trivy 
-works [ ] YARA works [ ] secret scanning works [ ] dependency auditing works [ ] 
-tshark works [ ] tcpdump availability assessed [ ] nmap works [ ] Scapy imports [ 
-] OpenAPI tooling works [ ] Docker tooling works [ ] Kubernetes tooling works [ ] 
-CI tooling works ``` Anything impossible in the current Codespace must be 
-explicitly documented rather than falsely marked successful. ---
-# 32. AUTOMATED VERIFICATION SCRIPT
-Create: ```text .devcontainer/scripts/verify-toolchain.sh ``` and additional 
-domain-specific verification scripts. The main verifier should produce a table 
-like: ```text TOOL STATUS VERSION NOTES 
---------------------------------------------------------------- agy PASS x.x.x ... 
-node PASS x.x.x ... python PASS x.x.x ... java PASS x.x.x ... gradle PASS x.x.x 
-... adb PASS x.x.x ... jadx PASS x.x.x ... apktool PASS x.x.x ... ghidra-headless 
-PASS x.x ... radare2 PASS x.x.x ... rizin PASS x.x.x ... semgrep PASS x.x.x ... 
-codeql PASS x.x.x ... trivy PASS x.x.x ... tshark PASS x.x.x ... playwright PASS 
-x.x.x ... ``` The script must exit non-zero only when a **required** component 
-fails. Optional components should be reported without causing the entire 
-environment to fail. ---
-# 33. TOOLCHAIN HEALTH COMMAND
-Create a single command: ```bash agy-env-doctor ``` which performs: ```text 
-environment check dependency check agent check MCP check Android check 
-binary-analysis check security check network-analysis check web check container 
-check ``` Output: ```text PASS WARN FAIL UNAVAILABLE ``` and provide remediation 
-suggestions. ---
-# 34. REPRODUCIBILITY
-The environment must be reproducible. Prefer: ```text 
-.devcontainer/devcontainer.json Dockerfile Dev Container Features bootstrap 
-scripts version-aware installation verification scripts ``` over manually 
-installing everything once. Do not rely on untracked state where avoidable. When 
-downloading a binary: 1. verify official source 2. verify architecture 3. verify 
-version 4. verify checksum/signature where available 5. install 6. verify 
-execution Do not blindly execute unknown shell scripts downloaded from arbitrary 
-sources. ---
-# 35. IDE EXTENSIONS VS CLI TOOLS
-Maintain a clear separation: ```text IDE EXTENSION = editor UX / diagnostics / 
-    integration
-CLI TOOL = actual capability MCP = AI-accessible tool interface SKILL = domain 
-    workflow instructions
-HOOK = deterministic enforcement / automation SUBAGENT = specialized reasoning 
-    context
-``` Do not install a VS Code extension when a CLI tool is the real requirement. Do 
-not install an MCP server when Antigravity already has a better native capability. 
-Do not install filesystem MCP merely because it is popular if native agent 
-filesystem tools already provide the required scope. ---
-# 36. RESOURCE MANAGEMENT
-GitHub Codespaces resources are finite. Before installing heavyweight components 
-estimate: ```text RAM CPU disk download size runtime overhead ``` Do not 
-simultaneously run: ```text multiple emulators large Ghidra analyses multiple 
-Docker builds large CodeQL databases multiple browser instances ``` unless 
-resources permit it. Prefer: ```text headless on-demand cached project-specific 
-lazy ``` operation. ---
-# 37. FAILURE RECOVERY POLICY
-When something fails:
-### Step 1
-Capture: ```text command exit code stderr stdout environment ```
-### Step 2
-Identify the actual cause.
-### Step 3
-Attempt a supported remediation.
-### Step 4
-Re-run the original verification.
-### Step 5
-If still failing, try a documented alternative.
-### Step 6
-Record: ```text failure root cause attempted fixes successful workaround remaining 
-limitation ``` in: ```text docs/compatibility-matrix.md ``` Never hide failures. 
----
-# 38. DO NOT CORRUPT THE USER'S PROJECT
-This is an environment bootstrap. Do not refactor the application's source code 
-unless required to install its development environment. Do not change application 
-behavior merely to make tooling pass. Do not modify: ```text package.json Gradle 
-files application source backend source database schema CI workflows Docker 
-runtime behavior ``` unless necessary for environment integration. When 
-modification is necessary, minimize the change and document it. ---
-# 39. SECURITY MODEL
-This workstation is intended for: ```text authorized development defensive 
-security software analysis security testing owned/test applications authorized 
-network analysis ``` Maintain strong separation between: ```text analysis tools 
-production secrets personal credentials untrusted binaries ``` Never expose 
-personal browser sessions to automation. Never put secrets into Git. Never make 
-broad permission grants merely to make an installation convenient. Use the 
-narrowest practical permissions. ---
-# 40. VERIFICATION PROJECTS
-Do not rely only on `--version`. Create tiny self-contained validation exercises.
-### Web
-Create a minimal application and verify: ```text start server browser opens 
-Playwright interacts test passes console clean ```
-### Android
-Create or detect a minimal Android project and verify: ```text Gradle 
-configuration SDK resolution build APK generation APK inspection ``` Where 
-emulator support exists: ```text start emulator adb detects device install APK 
-launch application collect logcat ```
-### Binary
-Compile a small C program: ```c int main() { return 0; } ``` Then verify: ```text 
-file readelf objdump strings nm checksec gdb Ghidra headless radare2 rizin ```
-### WebAssembly
-Compile a minimal function to WASM and verify: ```text wasm validation WAT 
-inspection runtime execution ```
+# MASTER POST-BUILD AUDIT, CONFORMANCE, REPAIR & CERTIFICATION DIRECTIVE
+## ANTIGRAVITY MEMORY OS — PHASE 2
+### “Do Not Trust the Previous Build. Inspect It. Prove It. Repair It. Then Prove It Again.”
+
+---
+
+## 0. EXECUTIVE DIRECTIVE
+
+You are now operating on a repository where a previous agent has already attempted to implement a large persistent-memory architecture for Antigravity CLI.
+
+**Do not assume the previous implementation is correct.**
+
+Do not assume the previous agent understood the architecture.
+
+Do not assume that files that exist are actually implemented.
+
+Do not assume that tests that pass are meaningful.
+
+Do not assume that documentation corresponds to reality.
+
+Do not assume that a green status message means the feature exists.
+
+Do not assume that a script is functional merely because it is executable.
+
+Do not assume that an index is healthy merely because its database exists.
+
+Do not assume that memory retrieval is semantic merely because an embedding library is installed.
+
+Do not assume that contradiction handling works merely because the words “contradiction” or “supersession” appear in the source tree.
+
+Do not assume that candidate promotion is automatic merely because a “promotion” module exists.
+
+Do not assume provenance is trustworthy merely because metadata fields exist.
+
+Do not assume the Antigravity hooks are actually loaded.
+
+Do not assume MemSearch is actually wired into the real runtime.
+
+Do not assume a mock/demo/test path represents the production path.
+
+Do not reward the previous implementation for effort.
+
+You are the **second-stage engineering auditor, verifier, repairer, and certification authority**.
+
+Your responsibility is to determine whether the repository now contains an actual functioning Memory Operating System — not a collection of architecture-shaped files.
+
+---
+
+# 1. YOUR ROLE
+
+Act as all of the following, but keep their responsibilities conceptually separated:
+
+### ROLE A — FORENSIC ARCHITECTURE AUDITOR
+Determine what the repository actually contains.
+
+### ROLE B — SPEC CONFORMANCE ENGINEER
+Compare the existing implementation against the intended architecture defined below.
+
+### ROLE C — FAILURE ANALYST
+Find structural, semantic, integration, runtime, security, retrieval, data-integrity, and lifecycle failures.
+
+### ROLE D — REPAIR ENGINEER
+Make the smallest correct changes necessary to bring the implementation into conformance.
+
+### ROLE E — ADVERSARIAL TEST ENGINEER
+Try to break the system deliberately.
+
+### ROLE F — INDEPENDENT VERIFIER
+Determine whether the repaired system actually works.
+
+### ROLE G — CERTIFICATION AUTHORITY
+Issue only an evidence-backed final verdict.
+
+Your final completion status is not determined by whether you *believe* the architecture is complete.
+
+It is determined by whether the repository contains sufficient executable evidence to establish that the required properties are true.
+
+---
+
+# 2. ABSOLUTE RULE
+
+## THE PREVIOUS AGENT IS NOT THE SOURCE OF TRUTH.
+
+The repository is the source of truth.
+
+The executable behavior is the source of truth.
+
+The actual generated artifacts are the source of truth.
+
+The actual test results are the source of truth.
+
+The actual Antigravity runtime configuration is the source of truth.
+
+The actual MemSearch integration is the source of truth.
+
+Documentation is evidence about intent, not proof of implementation.
+
+Agent summaries are not proof.
+
+Comments are not proof.
+
+TODOs are not implementations.
+
+Function names are not implementations.
+
+Imports are not implementations.
+
+Placeholder return values are not implementations.
+
+Mock implementations are not production implementations.
+
+A successful script exit is not proof unless you establish that the script actually tested what it claims to test.
+
+---
+
+# 3. MISSION
+
+Your mission has exactly six stages:
+
+```text
+DISCOVER
+   ↓
+FORENSIC AUDIT
+   ↓
+CONFORMANCE GAP ANALYSIS
+   ↓
+TARGETED REPAIR
+   ↓
+ADVERSARIAL VERIFICATION
+   ↓
+CERTIFICATION
+```
+
+You must actually execute all six.
+
+Do not stop after DISCOVER.
+
+Do not stop after finding problems.
+
+Do not stop after editing code.
+
+Do not stop after running a superficial test suite.
+
+Do not declare success because “the architecture now looks right.”
+
+The final state must be an **operationally verified implementation**.
+

Your implementation must use the actual runtime contract rather than inventing a custom imaginary one.
